		<item>
		<title>Automate remediation post AWS DevOps Agent investigation</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/automate-remediation-post-aws-devops-agent-investigation/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Michele Scarimbolo]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 07 Oct 2026 15:46:49 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Advanced (300)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[DevOps]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Technical How-to]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">c4faaee6740a8b2bfe0bba8a988399d299da211b</guid>

					<description>AWS DevOps Agent can diagnose production incidents but is kept in observe-and-report mode so it does not change resources directly. This post shows how to use AWS Lambda Durable Functions, Amazon EventBridge, and Amazon Bedrock to turn its investigation summaries into pre-validated fixes an on-call engineer can approve with a single action.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;Reducing the time between incident detection, investigation, and remediation is a critical priority for organizations running production workloads on AWS. When an issue arises, on-call engineers often need to quickly diagnose the problem across application components, identify the root cause, and apply the fix, often in the middle of the night.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/devops-agent/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS DevOps Agent&lt;/a&gt;, an AI powered agent that autonomously triages incidents all day based on correlated metrics, logs, and application topology, addresses the first part of this priority by providing root cause analysis (RCA) and recommended actions for resolution. However, to retain control and help prevent unintended changes, organizations typically keep their observability agents, including AWS DevOps Agent, in an observe-and-report mode, where the agent diagnoses issues but doesn’t modify production resources directly. In this post, we demonstrate how to use &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/lambda-durable-functions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda Durable Functions&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of AWS Lambda, &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/eventbridge/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon EventBridge&lt;/a&gt;, and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; to create an automated remediation workflow that complements AWS DevOps Agent to complete the issue resolution step. This workflow transforms investigation summaries into pre-validated fixes ready for single approval action, helping you reduce mean time to resolution (MTTR) and free your on-call engineers from repetitive diagnostic work.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="solution-overview"&gt;Solution overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/lambda-durable-functions/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda Durable Functions&lt;/a&gt;, you can build resilient multi-step applications and AI workflows that can run for up to one year without requiring you to manage additional infrastructure or write custom state management and error handling code. These functions automatically checkpoint progress, suspend execution during long-running tasks, and recover from failures while maintaining reliable progress despite interruptions.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The following diagram illustrates the solution architecture.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-1.png" alt="Architecture diagram showing AWS DevOps Agent sending an investigation-completed event to Amazon EventBridge, which triggers an AWS Lambda function that invokes an AWS Lambda durable function; the durable function calls Amazon Bedrock to find and list remediation tools, optionally pauses for human approval, then applies remediations to the infrastructure" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Automated remediation workflow from an AWS DevOps Agent investigation through Amazon EventBridge, AWS Lambda, and Amazon Bedrock, with optional human approval before changes reach the infrastructure&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The workflow consists of the following steps:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;AWS DevOps Agent completes an incident investigation and emits an event containing symptoms, findings, and root cause analysis.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon EventBridge receives the investigation completion event and triggers the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-trigger&lt;/code&gt; function with the investigation content.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The Lambda function packages the investigation summary and invokes the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-remediation-durable&lt;/code&gt; durable function.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The durable function sends the investigation context to Amazon Bedrock, which analyzes the findings and looks for applicable remediations.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock identifies and lists the available remediation tools from a curated allowlist of approved Lambda functions: &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-lambda-tool&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock proposes specific remediation actions based on the investigation findings and the available tools.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;For read-only actions, the durable function runs the remediation tools autonomously. For infrastructure changes, the workflow suspends and waits for &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/lambda/latest/dg/durable-examples.html#durable-examples-long-running" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;human approval&lt;/a&gt; before proceeding.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;After approval, the durable function applies the remediation actions to the infrastructure using the selected tools.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The durable function runs as an &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/prescriptive-guidance/latest/agentic-ai-patterns/prompt-chaining-saga-patterns.html#agent-choreography" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;agentic loop&lt;/a&gt;, iteratively calling Amazon Bedrock, executing approved tools, and feeding results back into the conversation until the remediation is complete. To keep automated actions safe and auditable, the orchestrator enforces a curated allowlist of remediation tools. Each tool is a purpose-built Lambda function that performs a specific, well-scoped action, such as reading a Lambda function configuration or updating an AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) policy statement. Amazon Bedrock can only select and invoke tools from this approved set, which keeps the scope of automated actions controlled. The workflow further distinguishes between read-only operations and mutating operations. Read-only tools run autonomously without human intervention. Mutating actions that would modify infrastructure state cause the durable function to suspend execution and wait for human approval. This is where AWS Lambda Durable Functions provide a key advantage. The function &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/durable-execution/#key-features" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;checkpoints&lt;/a&gt; its progress and pauses for minutes, hours, or even days without consuming compute resources, then resumes exactly where it left off after it receives the approval signal. By the time the on-call engineer engages, the system has already gathered relevant configurations, correlated the root cause with available remediation actions, and prepared a set of pre-validated changes ready for one-click approval. The current implementation uses an approve or reject signal. Because the callback accepts an arbitrary JSON payload, you can extend the approval to carry parameter overrides or reviewer observations. These can be fed back into the Bedrock conversation to refine the proposed remediation before execution.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In the following sections, we walk through the implementation details, including the Amazon EventBridge rule configuration and the durable function orchestration logic. We then deploy the solution using the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cdk/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Cloud Development Kit&lt;/a&gt; (AWS CDK).&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="prerequisites"&gt;Prerequisites&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before deploying this solution, verify that you have the following prerequisites:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The &lt;a href="http://aws.amazon.com/cli" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Command Line Interface&lt;/a&gt; (AWS CLI) installed and configured.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Python 3.14 or later.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cdk/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS CDK&lt;/a&gt; installed.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;An active &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/devopsagent/latest/userguide/getting-started-with-aws-devops-agent-creating-an-agent-space.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS DevOps Agent space&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;(Optional) &lt;a href="https://kiro.dev" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kiro&lt;/a&gt; with the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/products/developer-tools/agent-toolkit-for-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Agent Toolkit for AWS&lt;/a&gt;. The Agent Toolkit gives Kiro secure access to AWS APIs through a managed MCP Server with IAM-based access controls. If you use Kiro, the incident simulation, deployment, and cleanup steps in this post can be completed with natural language prompts instead of running CLI commands manually. To set it up, add the AWS MCP Server to &lt;code&gt;~/.kiro/settings/mcp.json&lt;/code&gt; (&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/products/developer-tools/agent-toolkit-for-aws/#getting-started" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;setup instructions&lt;/a&gt;). The repository includes a Kiro rules and agents file that gives Kiro the project context, deployment sequence, and safety conventions automatically.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="simulate-the-incident"&gt;Simulate the incident&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To demonstrate the end-to-end workflow, we simulate a common scenario: a Lambda function that exceeds its configured timeout. This gives AWS DevOps Agent a real incident to investigate and sets the remediation workflow in motion.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To keep the focus on the remediation solution itself, the steps to create and invoke this test function are kept in the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-automate-remediation-post-devops-agent-investigation.git" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;repository&lt;/a&gt;. It includes a ready-to-use &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt; function and step-by-step instructions to deploy it, invoke it, and confirm the timeout error in Amazon CloudWatch Logs. For the full walkthrough, see the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-automate-remediation-post-devops-agent-investigation#simulate-the-incident" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;“Simulate the incident” section of the README&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After the function is deployed and has produced at least one timeout error, you’re ready to start an investigation with AWS DevOps Agent.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="deploy-the-solution-using-the-aws-cdk"&gt;Deploy the solution using the AWS CDK&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Complete the following steps to deploy the remaining solution resources:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Kiro:&lt;/strong&gt; If you have Kiro with the Agent Toolkit for AWS configured (see Prerequisites), open the cloned repository in Kiro and ask: “&lt;em&gt;Set up the Python environment and deploy the CDK stack. Show me what resources will be created before deploying.&lt;/em&gt;” Kiro reads the project rules from the repository, sets up the virtual environment, installs dependencies, and shows you the planned resources before deploying. It confirms each infrastructure change before executing, following the same human-in-the-loop pattern that the remediation solution itself uses. To deploy manually, follow these steps.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Clone the AWS CDK code hosted on GitHub:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ git clone https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-automate-remediation-post-devops-agent-investigation.git&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Navigate to the directory &lt;code&gt;sample-automate-remediation-post-devops-agent-investigation&lt;/code&gt;:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ cd sample-automate-remediation-post-devops-agent-investigation&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Bootstrap the AWS CDK. This is required the first time you use the AWS CDK in a specific AWS &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/cdk/v2/guide/environments.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;environment&lt;/a&gt; (a combination of an AWS account and AWS Region).
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ cdk bootstrap&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Deploy the stack:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ cdk deploy&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The AWS CDK automatically provisions and configures the following resources:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Three Lambda functions:
  &lt;ul&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;devops-agent-trigger&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;devops-agent-remediation-durable&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
   &lt;li&gt;&lt;code&gt;devops-agent-lambda-tool&lt;/code&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
  &lt;/ul&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon EventBridge rule.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The AWS CDK automatically handles the IAM permissions using least-privilege principles and AWS security best practices. For example, Amazon EventBridge is granted &lt;code&gt;lambda:InvokeFunction&lt;/code&gt; permissions for the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-trigger&lt;/code&gt; function. The stack grants the &lt;code&gt;aidevops:ListJournalRecords&lt;/code&gt; permission to the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-trigger&lt;/code&gt; function so it can fetch investigation summaries from the AWS DevOps Agent journal. It also grants the &lt;code&gt;bedrock:InvokeModel&lt;/code&gt; permission to the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-remediation-durable&lt;/code&gt; function so it can invoke Amazon Bedrock.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="validate-the-solution"&gt;Validate the solution&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With the remediation stack deployed and the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt; function failing with timeout errors, we can now walk through the end-to-end workflow.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="start-an-investigation-with-aws-devops-agent"&gt;Start an investigation with AWS DevOps Agent&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Open the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/aidevops/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS DevOps Agent console&lt;/a&gt;, navigate to your agent space, and ask: “&lt;em&gt;What is happening with the devops-agent-timeout function?&lt;/em&gt;”&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-2.png" alt="AWS DevOps Agent console with a prompt asking what is happening with the devops-agent-timeout function" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Starting an investigation from the AWS DevOps Agent console&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The investigation starts and takes a few minutes to complete. During this time, AWS DevOps Agent autonomously correlates CloudWatch metrics, logs, and the function’s configuration to determine the root cause.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-3.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-3.png" alt="AWS DevOps Agent investigation in progress, correlating Amazon CloudWatch metrics, logs, and the function configuration" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 3: AWS DevOps Agent correlating signals during the investigation&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After the investigation completes, AWS DevOps Agent presents the root cause analysis, identifying that the function timeout is insufficient for the workload.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-4.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-4.png" alt="AWS DevOps Agent root cause analysis identifying that the function timeout is insufficient for the workload" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 4: The root cause analysis identifying the insufficient function timeout&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="verify-the-trigger-lambda-execution"&gt;Verify the trigger Lambda execution&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The investigation completion emits an &lt;em&gt;Investigation Completed&lt;/em&gt; event to Amazon EventBridge.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The rule triggers the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-trigger&lt;/code&gt; Lambda function, which fetches the investigation summary from the AWS DevOps Agent journal. In the &lt;code&gt;/aws/lambda/devops-agent-trigger&lt;/code&gt; CloudWatch log group, you can see the parsed summary that is sent to the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-remediation-durable&lt;/code&gt; durable function, including symptoms, root causes, contributing causes, and investigation gaps.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-5.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-5.png" alt="CloudWatch log group showing the parsed investigation summary with symptoms, root causes, contributing causes, and investigation gaps" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 5: The parsed investigation summary in the trigger function CloudWatch log group&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="monitor-the-durable-function-execution"&gt;Monitor the durable function execution&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Navigate to the &lt;a href="https://console.aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Lambda console&lt;/a&gt;, open the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-remediation-durable&lt;/code&gt; function, and choose the &lt;strong&gt;Durable executions&lt;/strong&gt; tab. Choose the new execution to inspect its checkpointed steps.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-6.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-6.png" alt="Lambda console Durable executions tab showing the remediation durable function execution and its checkpointed steps" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 6: The durable function execution and its checkpointed steps in the Lambda console&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The durable orchestrator begins its agentic loop by sending the investigation context to Amazon Bedrock. In the first Bedrock call, the model analyzes the investigation summary and determines that it needs to inspect the current function configuration before proposing a fix. It selects the &lt;code&gt;lambda_get_function_configuration&lt;/code&gt; tool from the allowlist. Because this is a read-only operation, it runs autonomously without requiring human approval. The step result shows the current configuration of the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt; function, confirming a timeout value of 3 seconds.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-7.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-7.png" alt="Durable execution step result showing the devops-agent-timeout function configuration with a 3-second timeout" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 7: The read-only tool call returning the current 3-second timeout configuration&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="amazon-bedrock-proposes-the-remediation"&gt;Amazon Bedrock proposes the remediation&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With the current configuration confirmed, Amazon Bedrock proceeds to the next iteration. It reasons that the 3-second timeout is the root cause of the failures and proposes increasing it to 30 seconds. The Amazon Bedrock response contains both the reasoning and the tool call:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  ....
  },
  "output": {
    "message": {
      "role": "assistant",
      "content": [
        {
          "text": "Now I can see the current configuration confirms the issue - the function has a 3-second timeout. Given that this appears to be a test function for timeout scenarios (based on the name \"devops-agent-timeout\" and its association with \"DevOps Agent Test Infrastructure\"), I'll increase the timeout to a reasonable value that should allow the function to complete successfully. I'll set it to 30 seconds, which is a common timeout for Lambda functions that need more execution time."
        },
        {
          "toolUse": {
            "toolUseId": "tooluse_lcGHbWDxng5HiLpmhWagDS",
            "name": "lambda_update_function_configuration",
            "input": {
              "FunctionName": "devops-agent-timeout",
              "Timeout": 30
            },
            "type": "tool_use"
          }
        }
        ...
      }&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Because &lt;code&gt;lambda_update_function_configuration&lt;/code&gt; is a mutating action, the durable function suspends execution and waits for human approval.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/06/20995-1.png"&gt;&lt;img class="wp-image-140795 size-full" src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/06/20995-1.png" alt="cloudwatch logs output of lambda durable function for approval request" width="100%"&gt;&lt;/a&gt; Figure 8: cloudwatch logs output of lambda durable function for approval request&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Important:&lt;/strong&gt; The &lt;code&gt;investigation_summary&lt;/code&gt; sent to Amazon Bedrock, and the remediation it proposes, are AI-generated and should always be reviewed before approval. The human approval gate is the security control: the approver must inspect the full tool parameters (for example, the exact &lt;code&gt;FunctionName&lt;/code&gt; and &lt;code&gt;Timeout&lt;/code&gt; in a &lt;code&gt;lambda_update_function_configuration&lt;/code&gt; call) and confirm the change is correct.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Using the AWS CLI:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ aws lambda send-durable-execution-callback-success \
    --callback-id &amp;lt;callback-id&amp;gt; \
    --cli-binary-format raw-in-base64-out \
    --result '{"approved": true}'&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Using the AWS Console:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Navigate to the durable execution, select the pending callback, and choose &lt;strong&gt;Send success&lt;/strong&gt; to confirm:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 568px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-12.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-12.png" alt="Lambda console durable execution with the pending callback selected and the Send success option highlighted" width="558"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 9: Approving the remediation by choosing Send success in the Lambda console&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In the input field, enter &lt;code&gt;{'approved': true}&lt;/code&gt; and confirm.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 674px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-13.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-13.png" alt="Send success dialog with the input field containing the approved true payload" width="664"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 10: Entering the approval payload to confirm the callback&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h3 id="verify-the-fix"&gt;Verify the fix&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;After approval, the durable function resumes, invokes the tool Lambda to update the configuration, and Amazon Bedrock confirms the remediation is complete. The updated &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt; function now shows the new timeout value:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-14.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/05/ML-20995-14.png" alt="Lambda console showing the devops-agent-timeout function updated to a 30-second timeout" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 11: The devops-agent-timeout function configuration updated to a 30-second timeout&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The final step output (&lt;code&gt;bedrock-call-4&lt;/code&gt;) confirms the successful remediation:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
 &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-json"&gt;{
  "EventType": "StepSucceeded",
  "Name": "bedrock-call-4",
  ....
  },
  "output": {
    "message": {
      "role": "assistant",
      "content": [
        {
          "text": "## Remediation Complete
          **Issue Identified:** The Lambda function `devops-agent-timeout` in eu-west-1 was configured with a 3-second timeout that was insufficient for its execution time, causing timeout errors at exactly 3000ms.
          **Action Taken:** Successfully updated the Lambda function configuration to increase the timeout from 3 seconds to 30 seconds.
          **Verification:** Confirmed the configuration change was applied successfully. The function now has:
          - **Timeout:** 30 seconds (increased from 3 seconds)
          - **Status:** Successful update completion
          - **LastModified:** 2026-05-22T11:11:28.000+0000
          This remediation should resolve the timeout errors by providing the function with adequate time to complete its execution. The 30-second timeout provides a 10x increase from the original 3-second limit, which should be sufficient for most operations while still maintaining reasonable execution bounds for a Lambda function."
        }
      ]
    }
  },
  "stopReason": "end_turn",
  ...
}&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This entire cycle, from incident detection to automated fix, required only a single approval action from the engineer. The system handled diagnosis, configuration retrieval, remediation proposal, and execution autonomously.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="clean-up"&gt;Clean up&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Clean up the resources you created by completing the following steps:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Kiro:&lt;/strong&gt; If you use Kiro with the Agent Toolkit for AWS, ask: &lt;em&gt;“Clean up all resources from the DevOps Agent remediation demo: destroy the CDK stack, delete the devops-agent-timeout test function, its IAM role, and its CloudWatch log group.”&lt;/em&gt; Kiro removes resources in the correct order, confirming each destructive action before proceeding. To clean up manually, follow these steps.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Delete the AWS CDK resources:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ cdk destroy&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Manually delete the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt; function that simulates the incident:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ aws lambda delete-function --function-name devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Manually delete the IAM role and the CloudWatch log group of the &lt;code&gt;devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt; function:
  &lt;div class="hide-language"&gt;
   &lt;pre&gt;&lt;code class="language-bash"&gt;$ aws iam detach-role-policy \
    --role-name devops-agent-timeout-role \
    --policy-arn arn:aws:iam::aws:policy/service-role/AWSLambdaBasicExecutionRole
$ aws iam delete-role --role-name devops-agent-timeout-role
$ aws logs delete-log-group --log-group-name /aws/lambda/devops-agent-timeout&lt;/code&gt;&lt;/pre&gt;
  &lt;/div&gt;&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This post demonstrated how you can automate issue remediation by using Lambda Durable Functions, Amazon EventBridge, and Bedrock in conjunction with the DevOps Agent. The solution picks up where AWS DevOps Agent leaves off, transforming investigation summaries into actionable remediation steps that run with human-in-the-loop approval. This approach reduces mean time to resolution because, by the time the on-call engineer engages, the system has already diagnosed the issue, gathered current configurations, and prepared a ready-to-approve fix. Safety remains central to the design: the allowlist restricts Amazon Bedrock to only invoke pre-approved tools, and the human approval gate helps prevent unintended changes from reaching production without explicit authorization. The architecture is also inherently extensible. Adding new remediation capabilities requires only configuration updates to the tool registry, not code changes to the orchestrator. And because AWS Lambda Durable Functions suspend without consuming compute resources during the approval wait, the solution remains cost-efficient even when approval cycles span hours or days.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To get started using this solution, download the complete AWS CDK template from the &lt;a href="https://github.com/aws-samples/sample-automate-remediation-post-devops-agent-investigation.git" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;GitHub repository&lt;/a&gt;, and follow the steps in this post to deploy the solution in your environment.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We would love to hear from you. Share your experience implementing this solution, ask questions, or suggest improvements in the comments. You can also join the &lt;a href="https://builder.aws.com/connect/community/community-builders" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Community Builders&lt;/a&gt; program to connect with other builders and share your serverless architecture patterns.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>Building AI builders: Playbook for closing the AI knowledge-capability gap</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/building-ai-builders-playbook-for-closing-the-ai-knowledge-capability-gap/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Alicia Retzlaff]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 07 Oct 2026 15:44:27 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Intermediate (200)]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Thought Leadership]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">557ef20339b4ca724a00b2bc1be9a67b2c82c106</guid>

					<description>The biggest barrier to AI adoption isn't awareness. It's the gap between talking about AI and building with it. Here's the playbook we used to turn non-technical, customer-facing professionals into confident AI builders in six weeks, and how your organization can replicate it.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;The biggest barrier to AI adoption isn’t awareness. It’s the gap between talking about AI and building with it.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Professionals whose primary role isn’t writing code, but whose daily work increasingly depends on AI solutions, don’t need engineering backgrounds to start building AI capability. They need the right tools, structured support, and permission to fail. Here’s how we proved it, and how you can replicate it.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-problem-organizations-are-ignoring"&gt;The problem organizations are ignoring&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Your teams are already in AI conversations, whether they’re fielding questions from customers, evaluating vendor solutions, or identifying automation opportunities in their day-to-day responsibilities. Given the pace at which agentic AI tools have become generally available, most of them have never built anything with the tools they’re discussing.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;These teams, regardless of whether they sit in sales, operations, finance, or product, have watched the demos and completed the certifications. But when someone asks, “how does this actually work?”, they don’t have the answer that comes from having built with it themselves.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The cost is real: slower adoption, delayed productivity gains, missed opportunities to identify use cases, and a growing disconnect between what your teams &lt;em&gt;know about&lt;/em&gt; and what they can &lt;em&gt;implement.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We asked business professionals, people who work with AI tools daily but aren’t writing production code, what held them back with AI adoption. The answer wasn’t “I don’t want to learn.” 90 percent wanted hands-on experience building AI agents. They were missing the connective tissue and scaffolding to build safely. The following chart illustrates the most common barriers business teams face when attempting to build with AI.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 802px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/02/ML-21448-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/02/ML-21448-1.png" alt="Bar chart of top barriers to building with AI; customer readiness is the highest at 63%" width="792"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Top barriers business teams cite to building with AI&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;At a high level, they could talk about &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale with any framework or model (80 percent had explored it). But fewer than 1 in 5 had touched Strands Agents SDK or built with &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/lambda/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Lambda&lt;/a&gt; agents. They were opting out of hackathons and build events because they didn’t believe their technical knowledge was strong enough to compete alongside engineers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To solve for this, we designed a six-week structured program that pairs business professionals with mentors and production-grade tools to build working AI prototypes that can scale beyond the program. The goal: close the gap between conceptual knowledge and hands-on capability.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="what-one-team-built-the-proof-in-six-weeks"&gt;What one team built: The proof in six weeks&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Four customer-facing professionals who had never worked together participated in our program. None had engineering backgrounds. Six weeks later, they presented their prototype, WealthWise, a multi-agent AI financial advisory tool, that won first place.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;What they built:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Five specialized AI agents&lt;/strong&gt; delivering intelligent financial advisory: portfolio analysis, risk assessment, financial planning, market insights, and personalized investment recommendations.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Dual-server architecture&lt;/strong&gt; (&lt;code&gt;Node.js&lt;/code&gt; + Python Flask) with Amazon Nova models.*&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/strong&gt; for multi-agent orchestration and conversation memory.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Four Amazon DynamoDB tables&lt;/strong&gt; for real-time data persistence.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Live market data integration&lt;/strong&gt; for context-aware financial recommendations.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Sub-5-second response times&lt;/strong&gt; for complex financial reasoning.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The system features coordinated decision-making: Each agent independently decides which tools to invoke and chains multiple tools together, performing multi-step reasoning across portfolio data, market databases, and financial planning models.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;They attributed their success to five principles they established on day one:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Learning over winning&lt;/strong&gt; – Choosing challenging approaches over safe shortcuts freed them to experiment without fear.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Regular cadences&lt;/strong&gt; – Daily standups prevented isolation and caught issues early.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Start with a Minimum Viable Product (MVP), then refine&lt;/strong&gt; – Working end-to-end flow by Day 2, then iterate.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Engage in office hours&lt;/strong&gt; – Actively seeking mentorship when stuck.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Have fun&lt;/strong&gt; – Psychological safety before technical output.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Participants reflected on how the hands-on format accelerated their learning:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“The hackathon’s timing was perfect, and the hands-on format proved invaluable for AgentCore enablement – accelerating what could have been a lengthy learning curve.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt;
&lt;blockquote&gt;
 &lt;p&gt;&lt;em&gt;“Great teambuilding and AI learning project! I taught myself a lot and have additional empathy for customers who are new to AI solutions.”&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/blockquote&gt;
&lt;p&gt;* &lt;em&gt;Amazon Nova models are available on Amazon Bedrock in select AWS Regions. For the latest availability, see the AWS Regional Services page.&lt;/em&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-playbook-how-we-designed-the-program"&gt;The playbook: How we designed the program&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The following sections break down the program structure, each phase, and the design decisions that made it work.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="program-structure"&gt;Program structure&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The program runs over six weeks, with participants dedicating approximately four hours per week. We structured it this way deliberately: based on our program data, participants who completed a phased program retained three times more practical skills than those in intensive two-day formats. Professionals who don’t write code for their primary jobs need iteration cycles: time to struggle with unfamiliar concepts, recover, and build again before confidence becomes durable.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="phase-0-recruitment-and-program-setup"&gt;Phase 0: Recruitment and program setup&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We recruited participants through manager nomination, Slack, and email campaigns, targeting professionals who work with AI solutions daily but don’t build them: account managers, solutions consultants, operations analysts, program managers, and similar roles. Management buy-in came first. We briefed team leaders on the time commitment (four hours per week for six weeks) and framed it as an investment in fluency and conversation quality, not a distraction from their day-to-day delivery.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The program was orchestrated by four core team members plus mentors who ran it alongside their day jobs. That’s a key replicability point: this doesn’t require a dedicated program team, but it does require at least one person with enough organizational trust to protect participants’ time.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="phase-1-team-formation-and-kickoff"&gt;Phase 1: Team formation and kickoff&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What participants do:&lt;/strong&gt; Form teams of 3–4 with intentionally mixed experience levels. Teams define a problem statement tied to a real scenario they’ve encountered in their role.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What this produces:&lt;/strong&gt; A scoped project concept that can be demoed and team accountability structure.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why this matters before moving on:&lt;/strong&gt; Without a concrete problem to solve, enablement sessions become abstract. Teams who define their target use case first absorb technical content with purpose.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="phase-2-enablement"&gt;Phase 2: Enablement&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What participants do:&lt;/strong&gt; Attend live training sessions on agentic AI concepts and AWS tools. These are not lectures. Participants work inside the actual tools they will use in the build phase, completing structured exercises that mirror their project work.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What this produces:&lt;/strong&gt; Baseline technical fluency and a working local environment.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why this matters before moving on:&lt;/strong&gt; Teams who skip straight to building hit a wall at environment setup and foundational concepts. This phase avoids the most common drop-off point.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="phase-3-ideation-building-and-development"&gt;Phase 3: Ideation, building and development&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What participants do:&lt;/strong&gt; Design and iterate on working prototypes over multiple weeks. Each team is paired with a technically proficient mentor who provides a safety net, unblocking infrastructure issues and suggesting architectural patterns without doing the work for them.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What this produces:&lt;/strong&gt; A functional prototype that could be demoed to a stakeholder or customer.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why this matters before moving on:&lt;/strong&gt; The extended timeline allows 2–3 iteration cycles. Teams who built a prototype in week 3 had time to continuously apply new learning, tear it down, and rebuild better in week 5. That iteration is where the real learning happens.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="phase-4-judging-and-demos"&gt;Phase 4: Judging and demos&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What participants do:&lt;/strong&gt; Deliver live demonstrations evaluated across five categories: Business Impact, Technical Excellence, Reusability and Scalability, Innovation, and Presentation Quality.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What this produces:&lt;/strong&gt; Peer-validated proof of capability and a library of reusable prototypes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Why this matters:&lt;/strong&gt; The demo format mirrors what participants will do in real conversations with customers, leadership, and cross-functional partners. Judging criteria reinforce that &lt;em&gt;working&lt;/em&gt; isn’t enough. It has to be impactful, scalable, and clearly communicated.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="critical-design-decisions"&gt;Critical design decisions&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tool selection is the most important decision for builders who aren’t engineers. The wrong tools create friction that halts momentum. The right ones abstract infrastructure and meet people at their current level of expertise. We chose:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://kiro.dev/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kiro&lt;/a&gt; Integrated Development Environment (IDE) for natural-language development: describe what you want, and it scaffolds the architecture.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Amazon Bedrock for API-accessible foundation models available in that Region: no machine learning (ML) expertise required to get a working response from a model.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Strands Agents SDK for composable agent patterns: agents compose like building blocks.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/agent-toolkit/latest/userguide/mcp-server.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Model Context Protocol (MCP) servers&lt;/a&gt; and AWS Lambda agents to push teams toward deployable, production-grade architectures, not only local notebooks.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The core principle never changed: build something real that you could demo on short notice.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="managing-day-job-disruption"&gt;Managing day-job disruption&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;We deliberately capped weekly commitment at four hours and gave participants flexibility on when those hours happened. The most common feedback was that participants applied what they were learning directly to their work within the first two weeks, making the time investment feel additive rather than competing.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-results"&gt;The results&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The following chart compares participant self-assessments before and after the program across eight key metrics, including AI competency, tool adoption, and readiness to apply learnings with customers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/02/ML-21448-2.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/02/ML-21448-2.png" alt="Before-and-after bar chart of participant self-assessments across eight program metrics" width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 2: Participant self-assessments before and after the program&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Going in, participants cited “lack of real-world examples” as their top barrier to building with AI. Coming out, 23 percentage points (pp) more participants rated themselves confident in applying AI to practical use cases than expected to be at the start. Participants left the program with a working prototype, a code repository, and a reference architecture they could demo to stakeholders immediately.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;What they built and are bringing to customers:&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Multi-agent care coordination for healthcare independent software vendors (ISVs).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Content moderation and triaging systems.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Fraud identification and prevention.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Customer churn prediction and prevention.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Multi-agent orchestration for autonomous decision-making.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Kiro + Amazon Bedrock AgentCore demos for customer workshops.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-impact-from-observers-to-builders"&gt;The impact: From observers to builders&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;When our participants gained hands-on access to AI tooling, they moved from discussing possibilities to building working prototypes. The numbers tell the story:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Self-rated ‘Strong or Expert’ understanding of agentic AI: increased from 27 percent to 82 percent (+55 pp).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Feeling ‘Well or Extremely prepared’ to identify AI opportunities: increased from 41 percent to 85 percent (+44 pp).&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Participants with only theoretical or limited experience: decreased from 34 percent to 0 percent (removed).&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Here’s what investing in this unlocks:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;For your teams:&lt;/strong&gt; They stop being AI observers and become AI builders. They know what’s feasible, what’s expensive, and what’s a weekend build from direct experimentation. In our cohort, 52 percent identified specific customers who could benefit from what they built &lt;em&gt;during&lt;/em&gt; the program, and 87 percent expected to apply their learnings with customers within 30 days. The program over-delivered on practical use cases because it required them to create the examples themselves.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;For your engineering orgs:&lt;/strong&gt; When business counterparts can prototype and articulate architecture tradeoffs, engineering teams spend less time translating requirements and more time building. Technical builders get clearer intake, fewer misaligned requests, and partners who can pressure-test feasibility before a single sprint is committed. Consider: 82 percent of participants left with a strong understanding of agentic AI architecture. Tool fluency surged across the stack: Strands Agents SDK went from 20 percent to 80 percent adoption (+60 pp) and AgentCore from 39 percent to 85 percent, raising the bar on engineering judgment.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;For your customers:&lt;/strong&gt; They get partners who’ve navigated the same architectural tradeoffs they’re facing by having direct, hands-on experience. Conversations move from “let me get back to you” to live walkthroughs. Before the program, 44 percent of participants cited uncertainty about architecture patterns as a barrier. After the program, 90 percent felt well prepared or better to identify AI opportunities in real customer conversations.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;strong&gt;For your organization:&lt;/strong&gt; You get a replicable model that compounds. Our pilot’s expansion from one team to regional and now global deployment demonstrates the pattern: hands-on fluency scales itself. The program achieved a 100 percent recommendation rate, with 95 percent of participants saying the program met or exceeded expectations.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="seven-lessons-for-replicating-this-at-your-organization"&gt;Seven lessons for replicating this at your organization&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;These seven lessons capture what made the program work and what to replicate in your own organization.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="tool-selection-changes-everything"&gt;1. Tool selection changes everything&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Low-code, agentic experiences remove the “I’m not technical enough” barrier. This is a program design decision, not a procurement afterthought. If your tools require Python fluency, you’ve lost half the room before week one.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="structure-beats-intensity"&gt;2. Structure beats intensity&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A phased program (6 weeks) with clear milestones outperforms a two-day sprint for non-technical audiences. Expect the first week to feel slow. The compounding comes after.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="mentorship-is-the-multiplier"&gt;3. Mentorship is the multiplier&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Pairing non-technical participants with technically proficient mentors removes the fear of failure and accelerates learning. The mentor doesn’t build for them. They provide a safety net.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="build-something-real"&gt;4. Build something real&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Requiring working prototypes with code repos and reference architectures (not slides) forces deep engagement with the tools. You can’t fake your way through a live demo. This is what separates a hackathon from a training course.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="measure-before-and-after"&gt;5. Measure before and after&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before and after competency assessments make the impact visible and build the business case for expansion. Self-reported confidence is useful, but the shift from “limited” to “extensive” hands-on experience is what matters.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="make-it-replicable"&gt;6. Make it replicable&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;A well-documented playbook (participant guidelines, evaluation rubrics, mentor pairing frameworks, environment setup guides) turns a one-time event into a scalable program.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="create-psychological-safety-first"&gt;7. Create psychological safety first&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;When people aren’t afraid to fail, they build things they never thought possible. The winning team cited “learning over winning” as their top principle. Every team that thrived established trust before code.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="get-started"&gt;Get started&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The playbook, evaluation rubrics, and environment setup guides are available for teams looking to replicate this model. Reach out to one of the authors to learn more about bringing this framework to your organization.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Learn more about &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; | &lt;a href="https://kiro.dev/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Kiro IDE&lt;/a&gt; | &lt;a href="https://github.com/strands-agents/sdk-python" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents SDK&lt;/a&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p style="clear: both"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;

			</item>
		<item>
		<title>How Cornerstone OnDemand cut database diagnosis by 78% with Amazon Bedrock</title>
		<link>https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/machine-learning/how-cornerstone-ondemand-cut-database-diagnosis-by-78-with-amazon-bedrock/</link>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Derek Ziehl]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 07 Oct 2026 15:38:28 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Amazon Bedrock]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Customer Solutions]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">15fb4f1a6de54cf66fb9eb96e6a07de1ebd20734</guid>

					<description>Cornerstone OnDemand built Orion AI, a multi-agent system on Amazon Bedrock and Strands Agents, to turn database operations from reactive firefighting into proactive automation. A three-person team cut database diagnosis from 45 minutes to 10, a 78% reduction, in six months. See the design decisions other teams can reuse.</description>
										<content:encoded>&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://www.cornerstoneondemand.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Cornerstone OnDemand, Inc.&lt;/a&gt; (Cornerstone) is a global leader in workforce readiness solutions, serving 140 million users across 186 countries. The company built a multi-agent AI system that transforms database operations from reactive firefighting into proactive, self-orchestrating workflows. The system, called Orion AI, uses &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt; and &lt;a href="https://strandsagents.com/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Strands Agents&lt;/a&gt;, an open source agent orchestration framework from AWS, to coordinate specialized agents.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before Orion AI, Cornerstone’s Enterprise DataOps team would spend up to 45 minutes per database incident manually querying system views and cross-referencing logs before handing off across teams. With Orion AI, database diagnosis dropped from 45 minutes to 10, a 78% reduction. A three-person team delivered the system in six months.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;In this post, we walk through the operational problem Cornerstone faced, how they designed Orion AI, the outcomes they measured, and the design decisions other teams can reuse.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-operational-challenge"&gt;The operational challenge&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Before Orion AI, Cornerstone’s Enterprise DataOps team operated reactively, with four recurring pain points:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Database performance investigations took approximately 45 minutes per incident, spread across multiple tools and system views.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Database lifecycle workflows required 10 or more manual steps, from establishing connections to communicating status updates.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Reporting between the site reliability engineering (SRE) team and the data team ran on a 15-minute lag.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;Redundant and overlapping alerts created noise that buried the signals that mattered.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Together, these meant engineers spent their time on manual coordination instead of resolving issues.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="the-solution-orion-ai"&gt;The solution: Orion AI&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI is a multi-agent system where a coordinating agent (the meta-orchestrator) delegates work to specialized child agents arranged in a hub-and-spoke topology. Engineers interact with it through a web application, asking questions and approving actions in the same place they already coordinate operational work. The agents cover infrastructure monitoring, database diagnostics, database lifecycle operations, customer analytics, and knowledge-driven support.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Two tenets guided the build: data privacy using AWS controls under the &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/compliance/shared-responsibility-model/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;shared responsibility model&lt;/a&gt;, and deep integration with existing operational tools. Amazon Bedrock provided managed access to foundation models, and Strands Agents provided the orchestration layer.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="results"&gt;Results&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI delivered measurable improvements in both diagnosis speed and accuracy and freed the team from manual coordination. By automating manual bottlenecks and streamlining cross-team workflows, Cornerstone achieved the following outcomes.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;table border="1px" width="100%" cellpadding="10px"&gt;
 &lt;tbody&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Metric&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Before&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;After&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Improvement&lt;/strong&gt;&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Database diagnosis time&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;45 minutes&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;10 minutes&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;78% faster&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Manual lifecycle steps&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;10+ steps&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;1 interaction&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;70% reduction&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;&lt;code&gt;SRE-to-data-team&lt;/code&gt; reporting lag&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;15 minutes&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Instantaneous&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Real-time&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
  &lt;tr&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Redundant alerts&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;High baseline volume&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;Filtered&lt;/td&gt;
   &lt;td&gt;65% reduction (median)&lt;/td&gt;
  &lt;/tr&gt;
 &lt;/tbody&gt;
&lt;/table&gt;
&lt;h3 id="faster-performance-troubleshooting"&gt;Faster performance troubleshooting&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Reducing database diagnosis time was Orion AI’s largest single efficiency gain to date. Previously, engineers manually connected to the affected SQL Server instance, queried system views for blocking chains and wait types, cross-referenced logs to isolate long-running queries, and correlated evidence to form a root cause hypothesis. This averaged approximately 45 minutes per incident.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;With Orion AI, three specialized agents share the investigation, each owning a distinct stage. Beyond diagnosis, Orion AI carries issues through remediation: identifying the root cause, recommending the fix, and creating a populated Jira ticket assigned to the right on-call engineer. A four-step manual handoff collapses into a single interaction.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="automated-database-lifecycle-management"&gt;Automated database lifecycle management&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Tasks that previously required over 10 manual steps, from establishing database connections to running cross-system queries to communicating status updates, now run as a single natural language interaction. Orion AI identifies the right tools and data sources, runs queries across systems, validates results, and returns a unified response. From the engineer’s perspective, the work is reduced to prompting Orion AI and validating the findings it reports back.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="real-time-monitoring-and-reduced-alert-fatigue"&gt;Real-time monitoring and reduced alert fatigue&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI removed the 15-minute &lt;code&gt;SRE-to-data-team&lt;/code&gt; reporting lag, replacing periodic manual checks with continuous cross-system visibility.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI reduced redundant alerts by a median of 65 percent through deduplication, threshold filtering, and cross-signal correlation. For every 10 alerts previously generated, only 3–4 now reach engineers. Alerts route directly to the responsible teams without an intermediate alerting layer.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="key-design-principles"&gt;Key design principles&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Three decisions shaped Orion AI, and you can apply them to your own multi-agent projects:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ol type="1"&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Split agents by domain, not by task complexity&lt;/strong&gt;: Each agent carries a narrow set of tool integrations for one operational domain. This keeps a model’s context focused and improves the accuracy of tool selection, rather than asking one general-purpose agent to do everything.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Default to keyword routing, fall back to semantic search&lt;/strong&gt;: Predictable requests are matched by keyword for speed. Ambiguous requests fall back to semantic search for accuracy. This keeps latency low without sacrificing correctness on the harder queries.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Scope conversational memory to sessions and bypass it for live metrics&lt;/strong&gt;: Persistent memory supports multi-turn conversations, but operational questions always read current system state. Bypassing memory for live metrics helps prevent stale data from contaminating real-time diagnostics.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ol&gt;
&lt;h2 id="architecture-overview"&gt;Architecture overview&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI is deployed as containerized services on &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ecs/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Elastic Container Service&lt;/a&gt; (Amazon ECS). Users interact through a web application that routes each request to the appropriate agent, invokes the required tools, and assembles the response. The following diagram shows how these components connect within the AWS Cloud.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;div style="width: 810px" class="wp-caption alignnone"&gt;
 &lt;a href="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/02/ML-20695-1.png" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;&lt;img src="https://d2908q01vomqb2.cloudfront.net/f1f836cb4ea6efb2a0b1b99f41ad8b103eff4b59/2026/10/02/ML-20695-1.png" alt="Architecture diagram of Orion AI within AWS Cloud. Web Application Users and external integrations (chat, issue tracking, incident management, and metrics dashboards) connect to a TaskExecutor agent running on Amazon ECS inside a virtual private cloud (VPC). The TaskExecutor acts as the meta-orchestrator hub, routing to specialist spoke agents including a Database Diagnostics agent, a Session Blocking Analysis agent, and other agents. A Portal-Tools MCP server inside the VPC connects the agents to the Data Plane, which holds SQL Server databases and the DATAOPS API. Amazon DynamoDB provides short-term memory. Amazon Bedrock, outside the VPC, provides model invocation, Amazon Bedrock AgentCore memory for long-term memory, and Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases backed by Amazon Simple Storage Service (Amazon S3). Amazon CloudWatch and AWS X-Ray provide observability." width="800"&gt;&lt;/a&gt;
 &lt;p class="wp-caption-text"&gt;Figure 1: Orion AI architecture: a meta-orchestrator on Amazon ECS routes requests to specialized agents, which reach data sources through a Portal-Tools MCP server and use Amazon Bedrock for models, long-term memory, and retrieval augmented generation&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;/div&gt;
&lt;h2 id="architecture-deep-dive"&gt;Architecture deep dive&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The preceding design principles show up concretely in the architecture. The rest of this section walks through Orion AI’s core components.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="building-the-hub-and-spoke-pattern-with-strands-agents"&gt;Building the hub-and-spoke pattern with Strands Agents&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI expresses its topology with a handful of Strands Agents primitives. The &lt;code&gt;Agent&lt;/code&gt; class is the building block for both specialist agents and the meta-orchestrator. Each agent’s tools are ordinary Python functions marked with the &lt;code&gt;@tool&lt;/code&gt; decorator. This derives a tool’s specification from the function’s type hints and docstring, meaning there is no separate schema to maintain. &lt;code&gt;BedrockModel&lt;/code&gt; wraps the Amazon Bedrock model each agent runs on, and &lt;code&gt;MCPClient&lt;/code&gt; connects agents to external tool servers.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The topology:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The hub is a single Strands Agent acting as the meta-orchestrator (the TaskExecutor). It holds no domain tools — only routing tools (&lt;code&gt;find_relevant_agents&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;call_agent&lt;/code&gt;) and control-flow tools (&lt;code&gt;emit_plan_step&lt;/code&gt;, &lt;code&gt;emit_confirmation_gate&lt;/code&gt;) so it can reason through a request step by step.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;The spokes are independent &lt;code&gt;Agent&lt;/code&gt; instances, loaded lazily through a decorator-based registry. Each carries its own model, domain tools, and localized system prompt.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;At runtime, the hub invokes a specialist by name through the registered &lt;code&gt;call_agent&lt;/code&gt; tool. Each specialist runs its own tool-calling loop and returns synthesized text back to the hub, which assembles the final response.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="hybrid-routing"&gt;Hybrid routing&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI uses keyword-first routing with a semantic search fallback. Approximately 80 percent of queries resolve through an in-memory keyword fast-path in under a millisecond. When keywords are insufficient to determine intent, the system falls back to semantic search powered by &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/titan-embedding-models.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Titan Text Embeddings V2&lt;/a&gt; to route by meaning. For model availability by AWS Region, see &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock/latest/userguide/models-regions.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Supported models by AWS Region in Amazon Bedrock&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;When direct tool invocation isn’t appropriate, a fallback chain activates: &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/knowledge-bases/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases&lt;/a&gt;, the fully managed Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) capability, retrieves operational documentation so responses are grounded in approved procedures rather than generated without context.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="domain-specific-agents-and-their-boundaries"&gt;Domain-specific agents and their boundaries&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI uses 13 domain-specific agents. The three SQL Server agents illustrate how domain splitting maps to the stages of a real investigation:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Database diagnostics agent&lt;/strong&gt; surfaces blocking chains, wait types, and long-running queries, then translates technical findings into business impact with remediation recommendations. It answers, “what is happening and what does it mean.”&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Session blocking analysis agent&lt;/strong&gt; runs multi-step investigations with reasoning models to map blocking chains to a root blocker. It answers, “why is this happening and what is the root cause,” delivering prioritized recommendations from immediate session termination to longer-term architectural fixes.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Real-time SQL diagnostics agent&lt;/strong&gt; queries SQL Server instances directly through Cornerstone’s DATAOPS API for live blocking data and high-CPU session analysis. It answers, “what is true right now.”&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The remaining agents follow the same principle of narrow ownership: infrastructure monitoring, operational analytics, database lifecycle management, customer analytics, knowledge retrieval from runbooks, notification routing, compound query decomposition, Availability Group listener resolution, and model connection warm-up.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="tool-integration-and-service-connectivity"&gt;Tool integration and service connectivity&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI reaches data sources through two patterns:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;&lt;a href="https://modelcontextprotocol.io/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Model Context Protocol&lt;/a&gt; (MCP)&lt;/strong&gt; for tools that several agents share (for example, real-time SQL diagnostics). A Strands &lt;code&gt;@tool&lt;/code&gt; function calls an &lt;code&gt;MCPClient&lt;/code&gt; over streamable HTTP to a Portal-Tools MCP server, which reaches SQL Server and the DATAOPS API. Jira integration follows the same approach through external Atlassian MCP tools.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Direct SDK or REST API calls&lt;/strong&gt; for sources that don’t need the shared MCP interface. This includes metrics and dashboards, on-call schedules, and Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases through &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/sdk-for-python/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS SDK for Python (Boto3)&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;p&gt;This split means each agent uses the lightest mechanism that fits its source, while the MCP server centralizes the tools that several agents share. These connections run over TLS, and credentials such as the MCP token and REST API authorization are supplied per request, not embedded in agent code.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="conversational-memory"&gt;Conversational memory&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;&lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/bedrock/agentcore/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore&lt;/a&gt;, a platform to build, connect, and optimize agents at scale with any framework or model, provides the cross-session memory layer. Orion AI coordinates context through a central memory manager that reads from three tiers in parallel, each with its own timeout and token allocation.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI coordinates context across three tiers. Short-term memory keeps same-session context in &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/dynamodb/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon DynamoDB&lt;/a&gt;, encrypted at rest by default, paired with a rolling conversation summary that &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/ai/generative-ai/nova/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Nova 2 Lite&lt;/a&gt; generates asynchronously. Long-term memory provides cross-session recall through AgentCore memory, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, namespaced by user ID. On task completion, Orion AI calls &lt;code&gt;create_event&lt;/code&gt; to trigger automated extraction, summarization, and consolidation. A third tier, inter-agent memory, is an ephemeral in-memory scratchpad that carries findings across sub-agent steps within a single task.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The memory manager retrieves across the tiers within a 500-millisecond hard timeout against a 4,000-token budget, backed by a least-recently-used cache. When a request concerns current system state, agents bypass memory entirely and read live data, so stale context does not contaminate real-time diagnostics.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h3 id="responsible-ai-and-guardrails"&gt;Responsible AI and guardrails&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Because DataOps safety depends on domain-specific rules, such as which database operations are disruptive, the team built custom guardrail logic rather than relying on generic content filters. Orion AI applies controls across four layers:&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;ul&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Prompt-level safety constraints&lt;/strong&gt; injected into every agent’s system prompt, blocking dangerous recommendations (for example, terminating critical database processes) and enforcing non-disruptive methods.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;A human-in-the-loop confirmation gate&lt;/strong&gt; that pauses destructive operations until the user confirms within a five-minute window, defaulting to deny on timeout.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Custom guardrail modules for input validation&lt;/strong&gt; (length limits, prompt- and SQL-injection blocking), output sanitization (redacting secrets and personally identifiable information), rate limiting, role-based access control, and per-request cost tracking.&lt;/li&gt;
 &lt;li&gt;&lt;strong&gt;Routing-level protection&lt;/strong&gt; that blocks answering operational queries from memory, forcing live tool execution for current-state questions.&lt;/li&gt;
&lt;/ul&gt;
&lt;h3 id="observability"&gt;Observability&lt;/h3&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI uses standard AWS observability services for monitoring and debugging. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/cloudwatch/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon CloudWatch&lt;/a&gt; provides metrics and structured logging for routing confidence, latency, and agent invocation activity. &lt;a href="https://aws.amazon.com/xray/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS X-Ray&lt;/a&gt; traces distributed calls across agent executions for end-to-end request path visibility.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="lessons-learned"&gt;Lessons learned&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The design decisions that made Orion AI possible are ones you can reuse. Splitting agents by domain let each agent carry narrow tool integrations without bloating a single model’s context. Using keyword routing by default with semantic search as a fallback kept latency low without sacrificing accuracy on ambiguous requests. Scoping conversational memory to sessions while bypassing it for live metrics kept real-time diagnostics trustworthy.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;The team also made pragmatic infrastructure choices. They deployed compute on Amazon ECS because &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/bedrock-agentcore/latest/devguide/agents-tools-runtime.html" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;Amazon Bedrock AgentCore runtime&lt;/a&gt;, a capability of Amazon Bedrock AgentCore, wasn’t available at the start of the project, and they’re now evaluating it as a future migration option. If you’re building today, weigh the same tradeoff for your own stack: start with what’s stable and available now, and revisit as managed capabilities mature.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;h2 id="conclusion"&gt;Conclusion&lt;/h2&gt;
&lt;p&gt;Orion AI reduced database diagnosis from 45 minutes to 10, avoided 70 percent of manual lifecycle steps, and cut alert noise by 65 percent. A three-person team delivered it in six months on Amazon Bedrock. The patterns behind those results are ones other operations teams can adopt: domain-scoped agents, hybrid routing, and session-scoped memory with a live-metrics bypass.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p&gt;To get started with multi-agent orchestration on AWS, review the &lt;a href="https://docs.aws.amazon.com/solutions/multi-agent-orchestration-on-aws/" target="_blank" rel="noopener"&gt;AWS Solutions Library guidance&lt;/a&gt;.&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;p style="clear: both"&gt;&lt;/p&gt;
&lt;hr style="width: 100%"&gt;
