										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We’re proud to share that Microsoft has been named a Leader in the 2026 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Global Industrial AIoT Platforms. We believe this recognition reflects our vision for the future of industrial operations: moving toward intelligent systems that continuously learn, adapt, and improve business outcomes.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://nam06.safelinks.protection.outlook.com/?url=https%3A%2F%2Fwww.gartner.com%2Freprints%2F%3Fid%3D00ThR00000KSaBbUAL%26ct%3D261005%26st%3Dsb&amp;data=05%7C02%7Cv-estvirko%40microsoft.com%7Cda0bd83adb404caf4a0508df2333d129%7C72f988bf86f141af91ab2d7cd011db47%7C1%7C0%7C639268377774886250%7CUnknown%7CTWFpbGZsb3d8eyJFbXB0eU1hcGkiOnRydWUsIlYiOiIwLjAuMDAwMCIsIlAiOiJXaW4zMiIsIkFOIjoiTWFpbCIsIldUIjoyfQ%3D%3D%7C0%7C%7C%7C&amp;sdata=VadEtoozF5VpKy6RBq%2BbzdmjR5M41EenXe%2FATZEHNk8%3D&amp;reserved=0" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Read the full 2026 Gartner® Magic Quadrant™ for Global Industrial AIoT Platforms report </a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As industrial organizations modernize, they need a foundation that connects operations across cloud and edge environments. Azure’s adaptive cloud approach, powered by <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/solutions/iot" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure IoT</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/azure-arc" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure Arc</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/local" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Local</a>, <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/microsoft-fabric" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Fabric</a>, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>, enables organizations to connect operational assets, contextualize data, apply AI-powered reasoning, and close the loop between insight and action.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6ac7895d8dd95&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6ac7895d8dd95" class="wp-block-image size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-894x1024.webp" alt="Gartner Magic Quadrant for Global Industrial AIoT Platforms (September 2026) showing Microsoft positioned as a Leader for vision and execution." class="wp-image-54401 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-894x1024.webp 894w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-262x300.webp 262w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-768x880.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-1340x1536.webp 1340w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-1787x2048.webp 1787w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/10/Figure_1_Magic_Quadrant_for_Global_Industrial_AIoT_Platforms_highres-894x1024.webp"><button

<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-shift-towards-learning-operations">The shift towards learning operations</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The first wave of industrial digital transformation was driven by IoT, connecting operational assets and making industrial data accessible, at scale. That visibility remains the foundation, but on its own it is not enough. Industrial organizations are contending with skilled labor shortages, supply chain volatility, increasing customization demands, and growing operational complexity. Success now depends on turning operational knowledge into scalable intelligence that improves throughput, quality, resilience, safety, and sustainability.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This is where Industrial AI and Physical AI create new value. Industrial AI applies intelligence to operational data, identifying patterns, predicting outcomes, diagnosing conditions, and recommending actions. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-cloud/blog/manufacturing/2026/10/06/physical-ai-in-manufacturing-from-intelligent-machines-to-coordinated-operations/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Physical AI extends these capabilities into the real world</a>, enabling autonomous or human-assisted action that learns from results and improves performance over time. Together with IoT, they establish a business learning loop:</p>



<ul class="wp-block-list">
<li class="wp-block-list-item">IoT helps organizations understand operations. The people learn.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Industrial AI helps organizations reason over operational data, identifying patterns, predicting outcomes, and recommending actions. The systems learn.</li>



<li class="wp-block-list-item">Physical AI helps organizations act, whether through autonomous operation or human-in-the-loop workflows. By measuring outcomes and feeding results back into future decisions, the process learns.</li>
</ul>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The outcome is more than automation. It is a transition from connected operations to learning operations, where every cycle of understanding, reasoning, action, measurement, and learning improves the next outcome. As the loop compounds over time, productivity increases, resilience improves, expertise scales, and the business itself becomes smarter.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="grounding-the-loop-in-organizational-intelligence">Grounding the loop in organizational intelligence</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A learning loop must be grounded in the operational context unique to each organization. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/cloud-adoption-framework/ai-agents/governance-security-across-organization" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">By applying AI through secure, governed platforms</a>, organizations can capture and scale the knowledge embedded in their people, processes, assets, and operations, while maintaining control of the expertise and intellectual property that differentiate their business. As that knowledge becomes institutional intelligence, it can be applied across adaptive workflows that continuously improve from real-world outcomes, helping organizations turn operational data into a durable source of competitive advantage.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  To accelerate this journey, the recently announced <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/frontier-company">Microsoft Frontier Company initiative</a> brings together industry expertise and enterprise-grade AI engineering to help customers design, deploy, and scale AI systems grounded in their own organizational intelligence, so they can translate operational expertise into measurable business impact more rapidly.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="recognized-as-a-leader-in-industrial-aiot">Recognized as a leader in industrial AIoT</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Gartner defines the global industrial AIoT platforms market as a series of seamlessly integrated technologies that deliver industrial data aggregation and curation, device management of industrial assets, ease of integration with IT systems, and the ability to unlock the value of industrial applications and AI. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  We continue to help industrial organizations transform by focusing on the areas our customers prioritize:
</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="unified-platform-for-industrial-needs"><strong>Unified platform for industrial needs</strong></h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft’s industrial AIoT platform extends Azure from cloud to edge, providing a consistent foundation for managing and securing industrial environments while connecting data and AI across operations. Key capabilities include <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/iot-hub" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure IoT Hub</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/iot-operations" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IoT Operations</a>, <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/microsoft-fabric" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Fabric</a>, and <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/ai-foundry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Foundry</a>. Together, these capabilities help organizations make data-driven decisions, boost operational efficiency, and scale AI across varied deployment environments.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="comprehensive-management-and-security"><strong>Comprehensive management and security</strong></h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft offers a unified management and security plane where millions of distributed devices and assets, from factory Programmable Logic Controllers (PLCs) to autonomous vehicles, can be managed as first-class Azure resources. Devices and assets connected through Azure IoT Hub and Azure IoT Operations are onboarded as <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/get-started/azure-portal/resource-manager" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Resource Manager</a> resources, enabling deep integration with Azure Device Registry and Azure’s control-plane AI capabilities for governance, insights, and automation. Microsoft is advancing a <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/security/fundamentals/zero-trust" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Zero Trust security</a> roadmap across the device lifecycle, from trusted identity, certificate management, secure updates, and firmware risk monitoring to policy-driven deployments and integrated SecOps. Encryption, confidential computing, sovereign operations, and auditable patching help customers strengthen resilience and meet evolving regulatory requirements.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="governed-industrial-data-foundation"><strong>Governed industrial data foundation</strong></h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft provides a unified data plane delivered through <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/iot-hub" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IoT Hub</a>, <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/iot-operations" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure IoT Operations</a>, <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/iot/device-registry/overview-device-registry" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Device Registry</a>, and <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/microsoft-fabric" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Fabric</a>. The platform ingests OT, IoT, and enterprise data; transforms raw telemetry into structured, semantically aligned signals; supports Unified Namespace and ontology patterns; and makes governed, reusable, AI-ready data available across many different analytics and agent systems. Support for open formats and industry standards enables interoperable digital threads, digital twins, and AI-powered OT/IT workflows, while low-code and no-code options let subject matter experts drive the creation of data pipelines and analytics.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="agentic-industrial-operations"><strong>Agentic industrial operations</strong></h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft is advancing industrial intelligence through agentic operations. Agentic AI can operate in both cloud and edge environments. In the cloud, agents use ontologies such as <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/iq">Fabric IQ</a> to understand operational context, monitor conditions, and collaborate with teams through tools like <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-ie/microsoft-teams/enterprise">Microsoft Teams</a>. At the edge, agents run within Arc-enabled adaptive cloud environments, using local semantic models to reason over site-specific data and assets, enabling intelligent operations even during periods of intermittent connectivity. For industrial teams, this can support practical decisions: detecting equipment anomalies, recommending maintenance windows, and prioritizing responses to quality issues or production constraints.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="shaping-the-future-of-digital-operations">Shaping the future of digital operations</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Ultimately, the future isn’t about connecting more things. It’s about creating intelligent systems that can perceive, learn, reason, and act, where every machine, every process, and every action makes the next outcome better than the last.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">By combining connected assets, governed industrial data, AI-powered insights, and intelligent action, <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/06/02/ai-alone-wont-change-your-business-the-system-running-it-will/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">organizations can create systems that continuously adapt and improve</a> from real-world outcomes. We believe this is the future of digital operations: intelligent systems that learn and deliver lasting business value.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Microsoft’s robust partner ecosystem can help bring local expertise and tailored solutions to every industry, unlocking new opportunities and delivering even greater impact. Whether co-innovating on industry-specific solutions or scaling AI adoption globally, our partners are essential to helping customers build confidently on Azure.</p>

  Discover how Microsoft’s industrial AIoT offerings can enhance your operations:
</p>

		<title>Responsible infrastructure at hyperscale: Managing the full lifecycle of Azure hardware</title>
		<link>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/responsible-infrastructure-at-hyperscale-managing-the-full-lifecycle-of-azure-hardware/</link>
					<comments>https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/responsible-infrastructure-at-hyperscale-managing-the-full-lifecycle-of-azure-hardware/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Alistair Speirs]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Wed, 30 Sep 2026 15:00:00 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Compute]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[AI]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Datacenter]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=54217</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>Microsoft is advancing Azure cloud infrastructure with more efficient systems and Circular Centers that extend the useful life of datacenter hardware.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/responsible-infrastructure-at-hyperscale-managing-the-full-lifecycle-of-azure-hardware/">Responsible infrastructure at hyperscale: Managing the full lifecycle of Azure hardware</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Every new generation of cloud infrastructure creates two jobs. We must deploy more capable systems quickly, and we must manage the hardware they replace responsibly.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Even though these jobs happen behind the scenes, the results matter to <a href="http://azure.microsoft.com" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Azure</a> customers. Newer systems deliver more compute capacity from the space and power available in a datacenter. At the same time, the reuse, refurbishment, and recycling of older equipment can extend the life of valuable components and return materials to the supply chain, ensuring cost efficiency and reliability. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Managing this process starts early in the lifecycle, with the design of our silicon, servers, networks, and datacenters. It continues through years of operation. When equipment leaves active service, <a href="https://datacenters.microsoft.com/tour/circular-center/">Microsoft Circular Centers</a> help determine its next useful life.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://datacenters.microsoft.com/tour/circular-center/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Discover more about Microsoft Circular Centers</a></div>
</div>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  With new Circular Centers in Newport, Wales, and Sydney, Australia, this work now spans North America, Europe, and Asia Pacific. These facilities help us recover components, prepare space for new infrastructure, and get more value from the hardware and materials already in our system.
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="getting-more-from-every-rack-and-every-watt">Getting more from every rack and every watt</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Cloud infrastructure must keep pace with the work customers want to run. That means increasing compute capacity while making better use of the space and power available in each datacenter.
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Compared with early general-purpose systems, today&#8217;s servers provide significantly more processor cores, more memory capacity, and network bandwidth. As shown in the chart below, Azure infrastructure has become both denser and more efficient over time. Since 2014, cores per rack have increased approximately 13-fold, while the power required to complete the same task has decreased by roughly 90%. For example, a workload such as code compilation that consumed approximately 100 watts on early systems can now be completed using less than 10 watts. 
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  This increase in compute density means we’re able to provide more server capacity to support customer workloads using the same datacenter footprint. By delivering more useful work from each rack, newer infrastructure helps improve resource efficiency while supporting continued growth and scale to meet cloud and AI demand.
</p>


<figure class="wp-block-image"><img decoding="async" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/word-image-54217-2.webp" alt="" class="wp-image-54219 webp-format" srcset="" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/word-image-54217-2.webp"><figcaption class="wp-element-caption"><em>Source: Microsoft internal analysis using combined average data across general compute infra partners and Cobalt using industry benchmark performance such as </em><a href="https://www.spec.org/cpu2017/results/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>SPEC CPU report</em></a><em> and </em><a href="https://microsoft.github.io/VirtualClient/docs/workloads/specpower/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>industry-standard utilization markdown</em></a>.</figcaption></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/new-azure-cobalt-200-vms-deliver-50-performance-improvement-fully-optimized-for-modern-agentic-ai-workloads/?msockid=2c72ae4c199c6f663812b85718996ea8" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Cobalt</a> is one example of this systems approach. Our custom-designed cloud processor allows Microsoft to optimize silicon, servers, networking, storage, and software together. Cobalt 200 delivers up to 50% higher performance than Cobalt 100 and incorporates the latest Microsoft security, networking, and storage technologies.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  This constant cycle of innovation is one of the defining characteristics of cloud infrastructure. As new generations of cloud and AI infrastructure are deployed, older hardware is gradually taken out of service. What happens to the equipment being replaced?
</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="giving-hardware-its-next-useful-life">Giving hardware its next useful life</h2>



<figure class="wp-block-image"><img loading="lazy" decoding="async" width="875" height="583" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/word-image-54217-3.jpeg" alt="" class="wp-image-54220" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/word-image-54217-3.jpeg 875w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/word-image-54217-3-300x200.jpeg 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/word-image-54217-3-768x512.jpeg 768w" sizes="auto, (max-width: 875px) 100vw, 875px" /></figure>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  Last year, Microsoft achieved a 92% reuse and recycling rate for decommissioned servers and components. However, at the scale we operate, retiring a server is a little more complex than simply sorting components into the correct recycling bin. Every server must be securely decommissioned, and data-bearing components wiped. Then we must decide how to recover the most value from it. Can the complete system serve another purpose? Can processors, memory, and other components keep working elsewhere? Which materials can return to the supply chain?
</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph"><a href="https://datacenters.microsoft.com/tour/circular-center/" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Circular Centers</a> manage these decisions as part of our datacenter operations. At these facilities, our staff give decommissioned hardware a series of possible next lives. Complete systems can support Microsoft labs and training environments, help schools and universities teach technical skills, or be sold to qualified buyers for continued use. Components can become spare parts for other systems. Materials are recycled when reuse is no longer possible.</p>


<figure data-wp-context="{&quot;imageId&quot;:&quot;6ac7895d95785&quot;}" data-wp-interactive="core/image" data-wp-key="6ac7895d95785" class="wp-block-image aligncenter size-large wp-lightbox-container"><img decoding="async" data-wp-class--hide="state.isContentHidden" data-wp-class--show="state.isContentVisible" data-wp-init="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on--click="actions.showLightbox" data-wp-on--load="callbacks.setButtonStyles" data-wp-on-window--resize="callbacks.setButtonStyles" src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-1024x576.webp" alt="Image of circular center facilities in a global map format. " class="wp-image-54322 webp-format" srcset="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-1024x576.webp 1024w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-300x169.webp 300w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-768x432.webp 768w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-1536x864.webp 1536w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-2048x1152.webp 2048w, https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-809x455.webp 809w" data-orig-src="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/wp-content/uploads/2026/09/Circular-Centers-Picture-1024x576.webp"><button

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Our Circular Centers operate as a connected global network. With recent launches in Wales and Australia, eight centers are now in operation across North America, Europe, and Asia Pacific. The network continues to grow, with a new center planned for San Antonio, Texas.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are also investing in technologies that improve the efficiency and scale of circular operations. Through automation initiatives with AI-powered capabilities, such as robotic disassembly and autonomous material handling, we are helping accelerate the recovery of components and materials.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="making-the-most-of-every-resource">Making the most of every resource</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">
  As demand for cloud and AI continues to grow, every part of the infrastructure lifecycle matters. We must get more compute from every rack and every watt, while getting more value from every server and component. Circular Centers help us do both. They make responsible recovery part of how we grow Azure, renew the foundational infrastructure, and build capacity for what customers need next.
</p>

		<item>
		<title>SQL Server on Azure Local is now generally available</title>
		<link>https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server/blog/2026/09/28/sql-server-on-azure-local-is-now-generally-available/</link>
					<comments>https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server/blog/2026/09/28/sql-server-on-azure-local-is-now-generally-available/#respond</comments>
		
		<dc:creator><![CDATA[Raj Pochiraju]]></dc:creator>
		<pubDate>Tue, 29 Sep 2026 16:40:06 +0000</pubDate>
				<category><![CDATA[Compute]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Databases]]></category>
		<category><![CDATA[Hybrid + multicloud]]></category>
		<guid isPermaLink="false">https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog/?p=54298</guid>

					<description><![CDATA[<p>With SQL Server on Azure Local, customers can modernize where their data resides while maintaining control over infrastructure, connectivity, and data placement.</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server/blog/2026/09/28/sql-server-on-azure-local-is-now-generally-available/">SQL Server on Azure Local is now generally available</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>
]]></description>
										<content:encoded><![CDATA[
<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Some of the world’s most critical databases run in places where cloud connectivity cannot be assumed. A remote industrial site, for example, may need production systems to continue operating when external connectivity is unavailable. A regulated organization may need sensitive data and processing to remain within sovereign boundaries.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, SQL Server on Azure Local is generally available for connected and disconnected operations. With this release, customers can modernize where their data resides while maintaining control over infrastructure, connectivity, and data placement. They can bring AI closer to their data with Foundry Local on Azure Local, currently in preview, and use eligible existing SQL Server licensing investments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Explore SQL Server on Azure Local<br>Run SQL Server where your data needs to stay<br>SQL Server has a long history of supporting mission-critical workloads on customer infrastructure. Azure Local builds on that flexibility by bringing Azure infrastructure to customer-owned environments, giving organizations a consistent platform for running SQL Server across datacenters and edge locations.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Customers can choose the deployment model that fits each environment:</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Connected environments: Run SQL Server locally on Azure Local, while using Azure Arc to manage SQL Server resources through Azure.<br>Azure Local Disconnected Operations (ALDO): Run SQL Server in environments where external connectivity is restricted, intermittent, or unavailable, with operations continuing locally.<br>This means organizations can choose the deployment approach that fits the requirements of individual environments rather than making the same connectivity decision across their entire data estate.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">SQL Server on Azure Local – connected and disconnected operations.<br>Bring AI closer to your data<br>Modernizing infrastructure is only part of the opportunity. Organizations also want to bring generative AI to existing enterprise data, including information that cannot leave their environment.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With Foundry Local, customers can run AI models on Azure Local infrastructure alongside SQL Server, bringing inferencing closer to the data. This enables organizations to explore AI-powered applications while keeping sensitive information and processing within their environment.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This can be particularly valuable in sovereign, regulated, and edge scenarios, where organizations want the benefits of AI but need greater control over where models execute and where their data is processed.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The goal is simple: customers should not have to move sensitive data somewhere else simply to begin building intelligent applications around it.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Build on your existing SQL server investments<br>Modernization should also make it easier to build on existing investments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">SQL Server is licensed separately from the Azure Local infrastructure platform. For connected deployments, customers can use eligible existing SQL Server licenses, including licenses with Software Assurance or qualifying subscriptions, or use pay-as-you-go licensing through Azure Arc.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For fully disconnected deployments, customers can use eligible existing SQL Server licenses through Azure Hybrid Benefit.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This gives organizations options to modernize their SQL Server environments while taking advantage of eligible licensing investments they already own.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">A foundation for sovereign and edge data<br>Enterprise data estates increasingly span cloud, datacenter, edge, and sovereign environments. The opportunity is not to force every workload into the same deployment model, but to give organizations a consistent path to modernization across them.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">SQL Server on Azure Local extends that choice to mission-critical SQL Server workloads, helping customers modernize close to their data, applications, and operations while bringing Azure-consistent infrastructure and local AI capabilities into those environments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">SQL Server on Azure Local FAQ<br>What is SQL Server on Azure Local?<br>SQL Server on Azure Local lets organizations run SQL Server on Azure Local infrastructure in their own datacenters and edge locations. It supports SQL Server workloads on virtual machines running Windows Server or Linux.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">What is the difference between connected and disconnected deployments?<br>Connected deployments use Azure connectivity and can use Azure Arc to manage SQL Server resources through Azure. Disconnected operations are designed for environments where external connectivity is restricted, intermittent, or unavailable, with operations continuing locally.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Who should consider SQL Server on Azure Local?<br>It is designed for organizations that need local control because of sovereignty, regulatory, latency, operational continuity, or edge requirements, including public sector, defense, financial services, healthcare, manufacturing, energy, and other regulated or connectivity-constrained environments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Can customers use existing SQL Server licenses?<br>Eligible existing SQL Server licenses can be used according to applicable licensing terms. For connected deployments, customers can also use pay-as-you-go licensing through Azure Arc. Fully disconnected deployments use eligible existing licenses through Azure Hybrid Benefit.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Can organizations run AI close to their SQL Server data?<br>Foundry Local on Azure Local brings AI inference to Azure Local environments and is designed to keep data processing on-premises. As of September 29, 2026, Foundry Local on Azure Local is currently available in preview, so availability and capabilities may change before general availability.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Where can I find deployment guidance?<br>Microsoft Learn provides guidance for deploying SQL Server on Azure Local, including hardware selection, SQL Server installation, monitoring, performance tuning, high availability, and related Azure hybrid services.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Get Started with SQL Server on Azure Local<br>SQL Server on Azure Local is generally available for connected and disconnected deployment scenarios.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">SQL Server on Azure Local – Overview<br>Deploy SQL Server on Azure Local<br>Deploy SQL Server on Azure Local Disconnected Mode<br>Learn more about Azure Arc-enabled SQL Server<br>Additional resources</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Manage SQL Server licensing and billing with Azure Arc<br>Foundry Local on Azure Local overview<br>Foundry Local deployment overview</p>
<p>The post <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server/blog/2026/09/28/sql-server-on-azure-local-is-now-generally-available/">SQL Server on Azure Local is now generally available</a> appeared first on <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/blog">Microsoft Azure Blog</a>.</p>

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">One of the defining strengths of the Fabric and SQL communities is how openly people share what they are building and learning. This week, nearly 5,000 data professionals are coming together in Barcelona to do exactly that. Across more than 130 sessions, more than 10 tutorials, community meetups, and roundtable discussions, attendees will exchange practical insights and real-world experiences that others can apply in their own organizations.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The excitement in Barcelona mirrors the momentum we&#8217;re seeing throughout Microsoft&#8217;s data portfolio. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Fabric</a> now supports more than 40,000 customers worldwide, and for decades <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/sql-server" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft SQL Server</a> has provided the backbone of enterprise workloads including more than 90% of the Fortune 500. Customers like Levi Strauss, H&amp;M, and Thomson Reuters are using these capabilities to unify their data estates, ground AI in their business context, and scale while remaining governed and secure.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we&#8217;re announcing new innovations across Microsoft Fabric and our database portfolio designed to help organizations power their Frontier Transformation, including deep integrations with the new Microsoft Copilot, agentic app development in <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/power-platform/products/power-bi" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Power BI</a>, end-to-end observability in Fabric, the database hub, and more.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Power your AI transformation with Microsoft Fabric</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="enabling-frontier-transformation-with-microsoft-copilot-and-microsoft-fabric">Enabling Frontier Transformation with Microsoft Copilot and Microsoft Fabric</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Software development has changed dramatically. AI is no longer simply helping developers write code faster. Agents are beginning to work across the full development lifecycle, from planning and coding to testing and deployment.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The opportunity now is to bring that same transformation to the rest of the enterprise. But organizations need more than models and agents. They need trusted context: governed data, shared business definitions, operational knowledge, and the guardrails that help AI act reliably.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This is the foundation of Frontier Transformation: embedding AI into core business processes, grounded in an organization&#8217;s unique data, knowledge, and workflows. Microsoft is helping enable this transformation through Microsoft Copilot and Microsoft IQ, which connect AI to an organization&#8217;s knowledge, data, applications, and business context.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">At the center of this system is <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/iq/overview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Fabric IQ</a>, a shared intelligence layer that connects enterprise data, business metrics, and operational context across the Microsoft ecosystem. By combining unified data from <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/onelake" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft OneLake</a>, trusted metrics from Power BI semantic models, and operational context from ontologies and real-time intelligence, Fabric IQ gives people and agents a shared understanding of the business.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="powering-the-new-microsoft-copilot-with-trusted-data-from-fabric-iq">Powering the new Microsoft Copilot with trusted data from Fabric IQ</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Millions of Microsoft 365 users rely on Microsoft Copilot every day to find information, create content, analyze data, and take action in the flow of work. With the introduction of the <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/09/25/introducing-the-new-copilot-with-home-code-and-autopilot/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">new Copilot last week</a>, it’s evolving from a conversational assistant into a unified experience where people can assist, delegate, and even build. Home brings Chat and Cowork together, while Code now gives everyone ways to build their own solutions. As Copilot takes on increasingly sophisticated work, access to trusted business context becomes essential. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we are excited to share we are bringing business context from Fabric IQ into Microsoft Copilot, grounding your work in a shared understanding of the business. Microsoft Copilot customers can now tap into AI-powered insights from Fabric as part of their Copilot experience, without additional AI token costs. Fabric IQ in Copilot Chat and Cowork is generally available today, with integration into the new Code experience coming soon through the Frontier program. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For Power BI customers, this semantic foundation is already in place. Business definitions, key metrics, and organizational knowledge captured in trusted Power BI semantic models and reports can now directly inform decisions and actions in Microsoft Copilot. This allows Copilot users to ask business questions and receive AI-powered insights grounded in the semantic data layer their organization already trusts.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As Bogdan Crivat highlights in his <a href="https://aka.ms/Analytics-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>Bringing governed analytics into the flow of work: Fabric Analytics at FabCon Europe 2026</em></a> blog, this is a key step toward conversational analytics that connects enterprise data, business context, and AI to help people make better decisions. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Check out the following demo to see this in action:</p>



<figure class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed wp-block-msx-ump-embed" class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed" data-bi-dnt="true" role="presentation">
	<div class="wp-block-embed__wrapper">

			document.addEventListener('DOMContentLoaded', () => {
				ump("ump-6ac7895d9ca7f", options);
			});
		</script>
	</div>
	</figure>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="the-next-evolution-of-power-bi-agentic-app-creation">The next evolution of Power BI: Agentic app creation</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With more than 425,000 customers across every country and industry, <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/power-platform/products/power-bi/?msockid=25495d82d1cc6b8a1d724a52d0f06afd" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Power BI</a> has helped millions of users turn their data into insights. Today, we are excited to announce the next evolution of Power BI: bringing agentic app creation to <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/power-platform/products/power-bi/desktop" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft Power BI Desktop</a>.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Business intelligence has traditionally followed a familiar pattern: analysts modeled data, built reports, and published dashboards for others to consume. Copilot in Power BI has made this process more conversational, helping users explore data and create and refine reports using natural language. Now, AI is enabling users to go beyond uncovering insights to build tools, workflows, and applications directly from their data.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The new agentic app creation experience in Power BI Desktop takes this a step further. Starting with a trusted semantic model, users can describe the application they need, generate it using natural language, preview and edit it in real time, and publish it to Microsoft Fabric without starting from a blank canvas.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">While Copilot in Power BI helps you analyze data and build reports, this new experience enables you to create purpose-built data applications. These applications can accept inputs, write back data, preserve shared state, and support operational workflows.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">This experience uses our new Fabric Apps capabilities to provide each application with a SQL database in Fabric, authentication, and security. As an application grows, users can expand its functionality and hand it off to developers and IT teams without rebuilding it.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Watch the following demo to see the new experience in action:</p>



<figure class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed wp-block-msx-ump-embed" class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed" data-bi-dnt="true" role="presentation">

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Importantly, this experience isn’t limited to Microsoft Fabric capacity customers. Apps in Power BI will be available in preview in the coming weeks to Power BI Pro and Power BI Premium Per User customers as well as Microsoft Fabric capacity customers. This means Power BI Pro and Premium Per User customers, at no additional cost, will also have access to Fabric Apps and Fabric Database capabilities—up to 1 GB per app—along with enterprise-grade security and scale for hosting. Fabric customers can then extend these apps with other Fabric workloads, creating richer applications on their existing data and services.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">To learn more about this feature and other innovations coming to Power BI, read Mohammad Ali’s blog, <em><a href="https://aka.ms/PowerBI-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Power BI&#8217;s next chapter: The evolution of business intelligence</a>.</em></p>

<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/power-platform/products/power-bi" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Explore Microsoft Power BI features</a></div>
</div>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="move-from-idea-to-production-faster-with-fabric-apps">Move from idea to production faster with Fabric Apps</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI can accelerate prototyping, but moving an application into production still requires teams to connect data, add business logic, manage infrastructure, and enforce governance. Fabric Apps closes this gap by giving developers a governed application layer on Microsoft Fabric.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Since the initial launch in June 2026, thousands of developers have used Fabric Apps to build AI-powered business applications, operational workflows, and custom experiences on governed data. Using the open-source Rayfin SDK and CLI, developers and coding agents can build application backends and deploy them to Fabric as Fabric Apps.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With the latest enhancements, developers can connect apps directly to Fabric warehouses, SQL databases, lakehouses, and Power BI semantic models without duplicating data, while preserving existing permissions and trusted business logic. New TypeScript backend functions, Secret Store, PostgreSQL support, private-by-default access, and built-in usage and performance metrics provide the services needed to take those apps into production.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities give developers a faster path from idea to secure, governed, production-ready Fabric Apps while spending less time assembling infrastructure and more time building differentiated application experiences.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Check out what you can build with Rayfin:</p>



<figure class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed wp-block-msx-ump-embed" class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed" data-bi-dnt="true" role="presentation">

<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Learn more about Rayfin by reading Sachin Patney’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/Rayfin-FabConEU26-blog" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>From prompt to production: What&#8217;s new in Fabric Apps</em></a>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="building-an-ai-ready-data-foundation-with-microsoft-fabric">Building an AI-ready data foundation with Microsoft Fabric</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">While Fabric IQ brings business context into Copilot, applications, and agents, that intelligence should first be created and maintained. Organizations need a foundation that can unify data across sources, transform and prepare it for analysis, and capture business meaning through semantic models and ontologies. Today&#8217;s Microsoft Fabric innovations make it easier to build this foundation from end to end.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="unify-your-data-estate-with-microsoft-onelake">Unify your data estate with Microsoft OneLake</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As organizations scale AI, connecting data is no longer enough. Copilots and agents also need business context, including terminology, relationships, instructions, and domain knowledge, to deliver relevant answers and take informed action. <a href="https://www.microsoft.com/microsoft-fabric/features/onelake?msockid=25495d82d1cc6b8a1d724a52d0f06afd" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Microsoft OneLake</a> provides an open foundation for connecting data across clouds, applications, and platforms through mirroring and shortcuts.</p>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading" id="share-governed-data-and-context-with-iq-sharing"><strong>Share governed data and context with IQ sharing</strong></h4>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we’re taking the next step with IQ sharing, now in preview. IQ sharing helps organizations securely share governed data and business context across teams, customers, partners, and ecosystems, making intelligence reusable beyond organizational boundaries. At launch, IQ sharing supports governed tables and files alongside Markdown agent instructions and RDF ontologies, with native support for Fabric IQ ontologies and Power BI semantic models on the roadmap.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">EY, S&amp;P Global, TomTom, Financial Fabric, and xMentium are already building integrations with IQ sharing. For example, TomTom is bringing its geospatial and transportation intelligence into Fabric, with available data products discoverable through the OneLake catalog.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, mirroring, shortcuts, and IQ sharing create an open data network that enables organizations to connect, access, and collaborate on governed data across teams, partners, and ecosystems. This extends OneLake beyond data integration to support reusable data products that power analytics and AI.</p>



<figure class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed wp-block-msx-ump-embed" class="wp-block-bloginabox-theme-ump-embed" data-bi-dnt="true" role="presentation">

<h4 class="wp-block-heading" id="expanding-the-onelake-ecosystem"><strong>Expanding the OneLake ecosystem</strong></h4>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We&#8217;re also expanding the reach of that open data network with the preview of bidirectional integration with Salesforce Data Cloud 360, the general availability of Google BigQuery mirroring, and deeper integration with Microsoft Dynamics 365 Business Central. Now, Salesforce data becomes part of the unified data estate without moving or duplicating it, making it available across Fabric for analytics and AI, including as business context for Fabric data agents. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Additionally, we are rapidly growing the OneLake ecosystem with Atlan, dbt, and ClickHouse, making it easier to bring trusted business context to OneLake data, transform it using familiar developer workflows, and work with it from external analytical engines. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities make it easier to work across platforms, tools, and clouds without duplicating data or building complex integrations. Learn more about all the innovation coming to Microsoft OneLake by reading Dipti Borkar’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/OneLake-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>FabCon and SQLCon Barcelona 2026: What’s new in Microsoft OneLake and its rapidly growing ecosystem</em></a>.</p>



<div class="wp-block-buttons is-content-justification-center is-layout-flex wp-container-core-buttons-is-layout-a89b3969 wp-block-buttons-is-layout-flex">
<div class="wp-block-button"><a class="wp-block-button__link wp-element-button" href="https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-fabric/features/onelake" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Get all your data in one place in Microsoft OneLake</a></div>
</div>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="process-and-harmonize-data-more-efficiently">Process and harmonize data more efficiently</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Connecting data is only the first step. Organizations still need to prepare, transform, and operationalize data at scale. To help teams move faster from data to insight and action, we&#8217;re introducing data engineering agent in Microsoft Fabric, and new Copilot experiences in Fabric Real-Time Intelligence.</p>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading" id="delegate-complex-data-engineering-work-to-data-engineering-agents">Delegate complex data engineering work to data engineering agents</h4>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As AI becomes part of the development process, data engineers are evolving from executing every task themselves to orchestrating intelligent systems. Fabric is taking the next step in that evolution with the data engineering agent, built on technology from <a href="https://blogs.microsoft.com/blog/2026/01/05/microsoft-announces-acquisition-of-osmos-to-accelerate-autonomous-data-engineering-in-fabric/">Microsoft’s acquisition of Osmos</a> earlier this year. Now in preview, the data engineering agent brings governed, autonomous execution to complex, long-running initiatives such as data platform migrations, lakehouse modernization, performance optimization, complex extracting, transforming, and loading (ETL), and data preparation.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Engineers define the desired outcome and establish guardrails. Within those boundaries, the agents then plan and execute the work autonomously, validate results and refine its approach, helping teams move complex data initiatives forward while keeping engineers in control.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Fabric data engineering agent is also designed for collaborative work that spans people and teams. Tasks inherit workspace permissions, enabling authorized team members to monitor progress, contribute context, respond to decisions, and work together on the same task.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">And because data engineers work across multiple development environments, these capabilities extend across Fabric and developer tools, including GitHub Copilot, Microsoft Visual Studio Code, Codex, and Claude Code. Engineers can start, monitor, and steer the same work from the tools they already use without losing context or progress.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Learn more about the Fabric data engineering agent in Bogdan Crivat’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/Analytics-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>Bringing governed analytics into the flow of work: Fabric Analytics at FabCon Europe 2026</em></a>.</p>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading" id="simplify-working-with-real-time-data-with-copilot">Simplify working with real-time data with Copilot</h4>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Organizations increasingly need to act on live operational signals, not just historical reports. New Copilot capabilities in Real-Time Intelligence help teams understand, monitor, and manage real-time data using natural language. Users can now create Real-Time Dashboards by describing what they want to understand, while Copilot assists users with eventhouse management and administrative tasks such as creating tables, configuring policies, managing schema, and inspecting cluster state.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">New schema recommendations and optimization capabilities further help organizations improve performance and reduce costs, while the operations agent accelerates troubleshooting with AI-powered root cause analysis and actionable recommendations. Learn more about Real-Time Intelligence innovations in Yitzhak Kesselman’s blog, <em><a href="https://aka.ms/FabricIQ-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Trusted AI starts with Microsoft Fabric, Real-Time Intelligence, and IQ</a></em>.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="curate-context-with-fabric-iq">Curate context with Fabric IQ</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">With data unified and prepared, organizations need a way to capture the business meaning, relationships, rules, and operational context that AI depends on. Fabric IQ provides this shared intelligence layer, extending governed business context across Microsoft Copilot, Microsoft Foundry, GitHub Copilot, and custom applications to help agents reason over a common understanding of the business.</p>



<h4 class="wp-block-heading" id="accelerate-ontology-creation-and-governance">Accelerate ontology creation and governance</h4>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we are making it easier to define and govern this context layer. We are introducing a new agentic experience to create <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/fabric/iq/ontology/overview" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">ontologies</a> from semantic models, OneLake data, and other context documents, reducing the effort required to capture business concepts and relationships.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">You can define rules in natural language, connect them to ontology entities, properties, and relationships, and make them part of the shared context people and agents use to navigate business operations consistently. You can also carry Power BI measures into your ontology, allowing you to reuse these established metrics.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We’re also affirming <a href="https://aka.ms/PowerBI-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">our commitment to Apache Ossie (incubating)</a>, a community-led effort to establish a vendor-neutral standard for exchanging semantic metadata across analytics, AI, and BI platforms. Working with Snowflake and the broader industry, we’re helping make semantic context open and reusable across platforms. </p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities make it easier to maintain shared business meaning and help agents reason over governed data with greater accuracy and consistency. Learn more about Fabric IQ in Yitzhak Kesselman’s blog,<em> <a href="https://aka.ms/FabricIQ-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Trusted AI starts with Microsoft Fabric, Real-Time Intelligence, and IQ</a></em>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="create-and-manage-agents-in-fabric">Create and manage agents in Fabric</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The value of a shared intelligence foundation comes when agents can use that context to deliver real outcomes. New enhancements across Fabric agents make it easier to build, govern, and operationalize AI experiences across Microsoft Fabric and other AI platforms.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We are deepening the integration between <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/data-science/concept-data-agent" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Fabric data agents</a> and ontologies in Fabric IQ, enabling more accurate and explainable answers grounded in business concepts and relationships. The <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/data-science/data-agent-creator-agent-overview" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">build agent with AI mode</a> can now use semantic models, documentation, glossaries, and data dictionaries to generate richer data agent experiences with less manual configuration. <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/fabric/real-time-intelligence/operations-agent" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Operations agents</a> are adding pre-approved actions and new root-cause analysis into preview, helping teams investigate issues, identify likely causes, and automate repeatable responses while maintaining human oversight and control. Learn more about these updates by visiting Bogdan Crivat’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/Analytics-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>Bringing governed analytics into the flow of work: Fabric Analytics at FabCon Europe 2026</em></a>, and Yitzhak Kesselman&#8217;s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/FabricIQ-EU26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>Trusted AI starts with Microsoft Fabric, Real-Time Intelligence, and IQ</em></a>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="operate-fabric-with-greater-visibility-and-control">Operate Fabric with greater visibility and control</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As organizations place more workloads on a unified data platform, operational maturity becomes as important as analytical capability. Teams need to understand what is happening across jobs, capacities, workspaces, and services—and resolve issues before they affect the business.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="introducing-new-observability-capabilities-in-microsoft-fabric">Introducing new observability capabilities in Microsoft Fabric</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Observability in Fabric, now in preview, is a new set of platform capabilities designed to help organizations move from reactive troubleshooting to proactive operations. Spanning workspace monitoring, the monitor hub, and operations agents, these new observability capabilities provide a unified view of the Fabric estate, making it easier to detect issues early, investigate root causes with AI assistance, and manage operations from a single experience.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">The new workspace monitoring experience allows you to consolidate telemetry in a single eventhouse for multiple workspaces, simplifying cross-workspace monitoring. You can even make the data available in other destinations with custom endpoints and control the exact retention rate and caching policy you need.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">We&#8217;ve also enhanced the monitor hub in Fabric with deeper visibility into jobs, capacities, and workloads through built-in views, alerts, and AI-powered insights. New Activator-powered job alerts notify teams of job failures and successes, while a new capacity view helps monitor consumption and headroom. In addition, we’ve added new investigation capabilities to operations agents, helping data professionals and administrators diagnose issues faster by autonomously identifying root causes and recommended actions.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these capabilities reduce the effort required to identify problems, improve reliability, and keep data and AI workloads running smoothly.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Check out the demo below to see observability in action:</p>

			});
		</script>
	</div>
	</figure>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="pay-only-for-what-you-consume-with-on-demand-billing">Pay only for what you consume with on-demand billing</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Visibility is only part of the equation. Organizations also need flexible, predictable ways to align infrastructure costs with usage as data and AI workloads scale.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Building on Autoscale Billing for Spark, on-demand billing expands consumption-based billing across Fabric workloads, allowing you to pay only for the compute you use, automatically scaling resources up and down based on your demand. Fabric zero-provisioned adds to on-demand billing by allowing you to get started with Fabric and OneLake without an upfront capacity commitment.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">To learn more about observability in Fabric, on-demand billing, and other innovation coming to the Fabric platform, read Kim Manis’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/Fabric-Platform-Barcelona-2026" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>Build, deploy, and govern Microsoft Fabric at scale</em></a>.</p>



<h2 class="wp-block-heading" id="manage-optimize-and-scale-your-database-estate">Manage, optimize, and scale your database estate</h2>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Building intelligent applications requires more than powerful AI models. Organizations also need databases that can scale with demand, tools to manage growing data estates, and intelligent operations that keep systems running reliably.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Today, we&#8217;re introducing new capabilities across Database Hub, Database Agents, and Microsoft Azure SQL Database that help organizations build, manage, and optimize the data foundation behind the next generation of intelligent applications.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="centrally-manage-your-database-estate-with-the-database-hub-in-fabric-and-database-agents">Centrally manage your database estate with the Database Hub in Fabric and database agents</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">As developers build more applications and operational workloads move into Fabric, managing a growing database estate becomes just as important as building applications. Organizations need a unified way to monitor, govern, and optimize these databases.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Building on its introduction at <a href="https://azure.microsoft.com/blog/fabcon-and-sqlcon-2026-unifying-databases-and-fabric-on-a-single-data-platform/?msockid=25495d82d1cc6b8a1d724a52d0f06afd" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">FabCon &amp; SQLCon Atlanta</a>, Database Hub in Fabric is now in preview. It provides a centralized control plane for managing SQL Server, Microsoft Azure SQL, PostgreSQL, Azure Cosmos DB, and SQL database in Fabric. From a single experience, administrators can monitor health, assess risk, enforce governance, and gain visibility across their entire database estate.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Additionally, we are introducing database agents for SQL and PostgreSQL on Azure, which will soon be available in preview. These AI-powered specialist agents, accessible in the Database Hub in Fabric and VS Code, continuously observe and analyze database signals, helping administrators investigate issues, understand performance bottlenecks, identify optimization opportunities, and accelerate troubleshooting. By bringing AI-assisted operations directly into a unified management experience, teams can move from insights to action faster while improving reliability, performance, and security.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Together, these innovations provide the operational foundation for intelligent applications across Microsoft, helping organizations manage their data estate with the same simplicity, scale, and intelligence that powers analytics and AI in Microsoft Fabric.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Learn more about the Database Hub and database agents in Shireesh Thota’s blog, <em><a href="https://aka.ms/SQLConEU26-databases-blog" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">SQLCon Barcelona 2026: Advancing SQL with greater control, scale, and intelligence</a></em>.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="bringing-greater-scale-efficiency-and-ai-innovation-to-azure-sql-database-hyperscale">Bringing greater scale, efficiency, and AI innovation to Azure SQL Database Hyperscale</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Intelligent applications place new demands on databases, requiring greater scale, efficiency, and built-in support for AI-powered scenarios. We’re introducing new innovations in <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-sql/database/service-tier-hyperscale?view=azuresql" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure SQL Database Hyperscale</a> designed to help developers reduce costs, scale demanding applications, and build AI-powered experiences using familiar SQL tools.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Serverless auto-pause for Azure SQL Database Hyperscale, now in preview, automatically pauses compute during periods of inactivity and resumes when workloads return, helping reduce costs for development, test, and intermittent workloads. We&#8217;re also increasing elastic pool density to support up to 50 databases per pool and making 192 vCore compute for Azure SQL Database Hyperscale generally available, enabling greater scale and concurrency for mission-critical applications.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">In addition, we’re announcing the general availability of DiskANN vector indexes for Azure SQL Database Hyperscale, Azure SQL Managed Instance, and SQL database in Fabric, bringing high-performance vector search directly into the database engine. Developers can build recommendation engines, semantic search, and retrieval-augmented generation (RAG) applications without moving data to a separate vector store.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="enable-sovereign-data-across-customer-controlled-environments">Enable sovereign data across customer-controlled environments</h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Organizations need the flexibility to run data platforms where performance, regulatory, and operational requirements demand. Microsoft is expanding where customers can run Azure and SQL Server capabilities, enabling modern workloads across sovereign, customer-controlled data estates—whether on the cloud, edge, or in disconnected environments.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">SQL Server on <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-local/overview?view=azloc-2609" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Local</a> is now generally available, bringing SQL Server and Azure capabilities to local environments. Organizations can run mission-critical workloads closer to their applications and operations for low-latency, high-performance access to data while maintaining control over where data resides. In addition, SQL Server on <a href="https://learn.microsoft.com/azure/azure-local/manage/disconnected-operations-overview?view=azloc-2609" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Azure Local Disconnected Operations (ALDO)</a> is also now generally available, enabling the same platform and experience in environments where cloud connectivity is unavailable, restricted, or intentionally disconnected.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Whether supporting regulated industries, operating at the edge, or running business-critical systems in disconnected environments, customers can now bring modern Azure and database capabilities closer to their data while maintaining the performance, control, and flexibility their workloads require.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Finally, Microsoft Fabric is expanding to Microsoft 365 Government Community Cloud High (GCC High) customers, supporting organizations that require United States sovereign cloud environments and compliance.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">Learn more about all the innovation we are bringing to our database offerings by reading Shireesh Thota’s blog, <a href="https://aka.ms/SQLConEU26-databases-blog" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener"><em>SQLCon Barcelona 2026: Advancing SQL with greater control, scale, and intelligence</em></a>.</p>



<h3 class="wp-block-heading" id="discover-more-fabric-and-azure-database-innovation">Discover more Fabric and Azure Database innovation </h3>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">AI transformation will not be defined by how many agents organizations create, but by how effectively those agents understand the business and work together to deliver outcomes. The organizations that build a trusted, connected data foundation now will be best positioned to lead.</p>



<p class="wp-block-paragraph">For a deeper look at the updates above and to see what else is new this month, visit the <a href="https://aka.ms/Fabric-September-26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Fabric September 2026 Feature Summary blog</a>, the <a href="https://aka.ms/PowerBI-September-26" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Power BI September 2026 Feature Summary blog</a>, and the latest posts on the <a href="https://blog.fabric.microsoft.com/en-US/blog/" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Fabric Updates channel</a> and the <a href="https://techcommunity.microsoft.com/category/azuredatabases" target="_blank" rel="noreferrer noopener">Azure Data Tech Community blog channel</a>.</p>
